# Number Ledger — WP / EC / D allocation lock

The append-only reservation ledger that stops two concurrent sessions from
allocating the **same** WP / EC / D number (the failure that renumbered WP-419 →
WP-421). See **D-24245** (this mechanism; renumbered from D-24242 — see the D
section below) and the allocation protocol in
[`01.0a-wp-drafting-phase.md`](REFERENCE/01.0a-wp-drafting-phase.md).
- D-24365 — arena-client-vfx-foundation-decisions (RESERVED: locks the VFX foundation's cross-cutting choices — (1) canvas-confetti@^1.9.3 [MIT] bursts + hand-rolled CSS/WAAPI word/shake/vignette, no tsparticles/GSAP; (2) a persisted UNIFIED Effect-Intensity control [governs visual intensity/off AND audio mute/volume via useAudioSettings] + day-one prefers-reduced-motion [client has neither today]; shouldRender('shake'|'particles'|'word') narrow union; (3) call-out flash at small, word at medium [Team-Up!/Unstoppable!/LEGENDARY!]; (4) DETERMINISM EXEMPTION mirroring D-24085: the client-app Math.random/Date.now/performance.now ban does NOT apply to the non-replay-bearing VFX presentation layer [src/vfx/, VfxOverlay] — it may use canvas-confetti [Math.random] + requestAnimationFrame; recorded here + a §client-app note in 02-CODE-CATEGORIES.md. All pure client-side reaction to projected UIState, hash-excluded. Landed at WP-556 execution; 2026-08-15, WP-556)
- D-24366 — menace-signal-uistate-contract (RESERVED: locks the Menace Signal contract — (1) the DENOMINATOR RESOLUTION ORDER for a scheme's loss progress: resourceLossCondition threshold when the scheme declares one [D-24315 suppresses the twist proxy entirely], else lossThresholdByPlayerCount keyed on lobby.requiredPlayers, else lossThreshold, else the MVP_SCHEME_TWIST_THRESHOLD default [D-24178]; (2) menace is a DERIVED PROJECTION computed in uiState.build.ts from G, never a G field and never persisted — so it adds no state-hash surface and needs no finalStateHash / PRE_WP080 re-pin; (3) the MenaceTier band boundaries are a SHARED CONTRACT locked once for BOTH future consumers [the danger-meter HUD and the adaptive danger-meter music channel], mirroring the D-24246 Combo Tier Contract precedent so the meter and the score can never disagree about 'critical'; (4) escapedVillains is NOT a generic menace input — D-24317 retired the generic ESCAPE_LIMIT loss, so escapes advance menace ONLY through a scheme's own escaped-pile resourceLossCondition, and the ewiki sound-effects.md escapeProgress = escapedVillains / ESCAPE_LIMIT formula is corrected in lockstep; (5) a scheme with an unresolvable denominator projects menace as the discrete-escalation fallback rather than a wrong ratio. Landed at WP-557 execution; 2026-08-16, WP-557)
- D-24367 — danger-meter-hud-display-contract (RESERVED: locks the client half of the danger meter — (1) the METER IS INFORMATION, NOT DECORATION: it always renders, and the WP-556 Effect-Intensity control plus prefers-reduced-motion gate only its ANIMATION [the critical-tier pulse and the colour/width transition], never its presence or its numbers; hiding live loss-progress behind an effects toggle would make a player turn off effects and lose game state, so shouldRender's shake/particles/word union is NOT extended and NOT consulted for the readout itself; (2) the HUD reads UIState.progress.menace / menaceTier / schemeLossProgress / schemeLossThreshold and NEVER re-derives a threshold — the D-24366 resolution order stays engine-side and the client owns zero copy of it; (3) the hardcoded scheme-twist-threshold=8 prop on TopHudBar is REMOVED [both PlayDesktop.vue and PlayMobile.vue pass it today], so the twist readout stops printing a denominator that is wrong for the unconfigured fallback [7], for Super Hero Civil War [8 at 2-3p, 5 at 4-5p] and for every resourceLossCondition scheme; (4) NO-DENOMINATOR RENDERING: a pile-depleted scheme omits schemeLossThreshold [D-24366 section 5], so the HUD renders the bare progress count and the menace bar WITHOUT a ratio label rather than inventing or defaulting a denominator; (5) tier -> visual treatment is a presentation-only mapping owned by the client, but the BOUNDARIES are the engine's MenaceTier and are never re-banded client-side. Landed at WP-558 execution; 2026-08-16, WP-558)
- D-24368 - hero-ledger-subsystem-covered-status (RESERVED: records that the WP-548 `subsystem` status is a TWO-LEDGER contract, not a villain-only one, and locks the granularity split - CARD-keyed for villains, where `subsystem` replaces a whole-card would-be-`(unmarked)` row, versus (CARD x MECHANIC)-keyed for heroes, where a single card legitimately mixes subsystem-implemented and genuinely-unsupported mechanics. Landed at WP-559 execution; 2026-08-16, WP-559)
- D-24369 — adaptive-danger-music-channel (RESERVED: locks packet 3 of the danger-meter arc — (1) A SEPARATE MUSIC ENGINE, not an extension of the shipped SFX engine: audioEngine.ts is strictly fire-and-forget [its HowlLike exposes only play + volume; there is no stop, loop or fade anywhere in apps/arena-client/src/audio], and widening that contract with loop/stop/fade would burden every one-shot SFX call site for no SFX benefit, so music gets its own musicEngine.ts alongside it; (2) HORIZONTAL RE-SEQUENCING, not vertical layering — crossfade between three independently-composed CC0 loops as menaceTier changes, because vertical stem layering requires stems recorded in sync which do not exist and cannot be assembled from independent CC0 tracks; (3) TIER-DRIVEN, NEVER RE-BANDED: the channel consumes UIState.progress.menaceTier and switches on tier CHANGE, never re-deriving bands from the menace scalar — the same discipline D-24367 section 2 locks for the visual meter, so the meter and the score can never disagree about critical; (4) MUSIC IS DECORATION, the deliberate INVERSE of the meter [D-24367 section 1]: it defaults ON at a lower volume than SFX, carries its OWN persisted toggle, and the master mute silences it — a player muting expects silence, and unlike loss-progress a soundtrack carries no game state, so gating it costs nothing; (5) the loop STOPS at gameover rather than playing under the endgame panel; (6) assets are three CC0 loopable tracks under a NEW audio/music/ R2 prefix, uploaded with the existing content-driven scripts/upload-move-sfx-to-r2.mjs [which already names the adaptive-music work as an anticipated consumer] — never committed to git. Landed at WP-560 execution; 2026-08-16, WP-560)
- D-24371 — scheme-faithful-loss-progress (RESERVED: CORRECTS D-24366 section 5, which was wrong. It reasoned that a pile-depleted scheme has no denominator because the pile's starting size is not a scheme CONFIG constant, and fell back to counting twists — so Super Hero Civil War, whose printed Evil Wins is "If the Hero Deck runs out", showed a danger meter measuring TWISTS. Observed live at gitSha 8eb8b0c: menace 0.4286 = 3/7 twists while the Hero Deck sat at 11. The starting size is not in the config but it IS knowable at setup; "not in the config" was conflated with "unknowable". Locks: (1) a scheme's loss progress is measured against ITS OWN Evil Wins condition — pile-depleted measures the named pile's DEPLETION against its setup size, escaped-pile-count measures matching escaped entries, and only a true twist-loss scheme measures twists; (2) the setup size is captured into G LAZILY — written only when the active scheme declares a pile-depleted condition — so a match on any other scheme carries no new field and neither sentinel hash oracle moves; (3) the engine projects a schemeLossKind ENUM (not a label) plus the twist threshold as a SEPARATE field, so the HUD can render both "Twists: 3/8" and a scheme-appropriate meter while the engine stays free of presentation words; (4) the denominator for hero-deck depletion is the TOTAL HERO CARDS BUILT at setup (42 in a 3-hero core game — the full pool including the 5 dealt to HQ), OPERATOR-DECIDED 2026-08-17 over the drafting recommendation of the post-HQ deck size (37): 42 is the number a player counts on the table, and the HQ cards are recruitable rather than gone; (5) fixes the solo gap — lossThresholdByPlayerCount carries no '1' key, so 1-player Civil War silently took the arbitrary MVP fallback 7 instead of mirroring 2-player 8. Landed at WP-562 execution; 2026-08-17, WP-562)
- D-24372 — engine-test-typecheck-gate (RESERVED: packages/game-engine test files are NEVER typechecked — tsconfig.json excludes src/**/*.test.ts and the package has only build + test scripts, so tsc never sees a test file and tsx transpiles without checking. Every satisfies-based "compile-time drift pin" in the engine suite is therefore DOCUMENTATION, not a gate — including pins in ui/uiState.types.drift.test.ts whose comments explicitly claim compile-time enforcement they do not get. Discovered during WP-557, worked around locally with a runtime keyset assertion, and named as its first post-mortem follow-up. Locks: (1) a SEPARATE tsconfig.test.json extending the base with the test exclusion removed and noEmit true, so the shipped build's rootDir/outDir/declaration output is untouched; (2) a typecheck script wired into CI beside the existing gates; (3) the fix is TOOLING ONLY — pre-existing type errors the new gate surfaces are FIXED IN TEST FILES, never silenced with any-casts or ts-expect-error, and never by loosening the base tsconfig's strict/exactOptionalPropertyTypes/noUncheckedIndexedAccess; (4) if the surfaced error count is large enough to exceed the packet, the overflow is recorded and split rather than suppressed. Landed at WP-563 execution; 2026-08-17, WP-563)
- D-24373 - investigate-keyword-static-criterion (RESERVED: records the `investigate` mechanic contract read from CARD TEXT rather than the divergent keyword blurb [look at top two of your own deck, draw one matching card, bottom the rest], the deliberate STATIC-CRITERION-ONLY scope with its six deferred families named, the inclusive-OR reading of `and/or`, and the rule that a new hero keyword stales three derived feeds at once. Landed at WP-564 execution; 2026-08-17, WP-564)
- D-24370 - in-play-hollow-baseline-rebuild (RESERVED: amends D-24050. Records that the frozen in-play hollow baseline is only sound while it is measured on the SAME instrument as the live sweep, and adds the rebuild trigger - a material change in sweep fidelity [backdrop, shuffle, turn depth] REQUIRES a baseline rebuild in the same or an immediately following packet, or resolvedObs stays pinned to the old instrument while totalObs moves to the new one and the metric becomes structurally unable to credit new work. Also records the non-goal: a rebuild must never be used to move the headline percentage. Landed at WP-561 execution; 2026-08-16, WP-561)
- D-24374 - vp-icon-marker-mismap (RESERVED: records that `{ icon: 4 }` in the upstream card sources is a VICTORY POINT glyph, not piercing, and that ICON_SLUG_MAP mapping it to 'piercing' put the wrong resource in front of players on 35 card abilities across 12 sets. Locks: (1) the evidence standard used - ALL 22 upstream uses were read individually and every one is a victory-point context, so the mapping is corrected at the map rather than patched per card; (2) the defect is DISPLAY-ONLY - no engine path consumes the marker and variable-VP scoring was verified correct in a live match [Supreme HYDRA 15 VP = 3 + 3 x 4, reconciling 23 villainVP / 52 totalVP], so this changes what a player READS, never what they SCORE; (3) sets that bypass convert-cards-v15 [the 4 outliers and hand-authored co2e] are audited separately, which is why the generated count [35] exceeds the upstream count [22]. Found during a match review, not a sweep - the card was read against its printed text. 2026-08-17, WP-565)
- D-24375 - blocked-ability-message-misattribution (RESERVED: records that a single generic "Hero class or team synergy" line stood in for every hero-hook condition failure, and why that is worse than a vague message. Locks: (1) a blocked-ability log line NAMES THE CONDITION THAT FAILED - the log is the player's only window into why an ability did nothing, and a message that confidently misattributes the cause is worse than one that says nothing, because it sends the reader to the wrong card property; (2) the message stayed wrong through EIGHT firings in one observed match while being simultaneously correct for three other cards in the SAME match, which is exactly why it survived - it is right often enough to look right; (3) an UNRECOGNIZED condition type must not be reported as an unmet one - evaluateCondition's `default` returns false, so a data or parse defect currently renders identically to a working synergy gate; (4) this is a LOG-FIDELITY decision, not a rules change - no gate evaluation is altered and messages are hash-excluded, so neither sentinel oracle moves. Deferred from WP-562 section Notes and reported from a real match, not a sweep. 2026-08-17, WP-566)
- D-24376 - red-skull-tactic-onfight-resolvers (RESERVED: records that a mastermind's Tactics can be fully inert with no signal at all, and that this is the second half of the WP-497 framework rather than a new mechanism. Locks: (1) an unimplemented tactic's SILENCE is itself a defect - dispatchTacticOnFight's unhandled fallthrough logs nothing, so a player who defeats a Tactic and receives none of its printed effect gets no indication anything was skipped; resolvers log their effects; (2) the framework's per-ext_id dispatch is the right shape and is EXTENDED, not replaced - other masterminds' tactics stay deliberately inert until their own packets; (3) an interactive tactic line [Ruthless Dictator's top-three KO/discard/replace] carries the pending-choice freeze risk and is gated on shipping projection + prompt + bot enumeration together. Evidence standard: found by reading four defeated Tactics in a real match against their printed card text, with the miss proven from the diagnostics economy [recruit 5, not the 9 that Endless Resources' +4 would have produced]. 2026-08-17, WP-567)
- D-24377 - wait-and-see-conditional-semantics (RESERVED: OPERATOR DECISION 2026-08-17 that "If you [did X] this turn" is a whole-turn WAIT-AND-SEE condition, granting its effect retroactively when the threshold is reached later in the same turn. Locks: (1) the semantics, and that they apply to the ENTIRE "did you X this turn" family [31 ability lines across 17 sets] rather than to Surge of Power alone; (2) BOTH readings are recorded deliberately - the drafting recommendation was on-play evaluation, reasoning that Legendary resolves an ability when the card is played, has no continuous re-check machinery anywhere, and that the rulebook's freedom to choose play order exists precisely BECAUSE conditions are checked at resolution, which makes build-recruit-then-play the card's intended skill; the operator overruled it on the text of the card - "this turn" names a whole-turn window and attack is turn-scoped, so a late grant is coherent. The recommendation is preserved so a future reader sees a decision that was MADE, not one that was missed, and does not quietly revert it; (3) a condition that is NOT YET met is a distinct state from one that FAILED - it must not inherit WP-566's blocked wording; (4) re-evaluation must be deterministic, order-stable, and fire an ability AT MOST ONCE per play even if a threshold is crossed, lost and re-crossed. Note the drafting analysis could not cite a rulebook clause either way - the repo carries no rulebook text and keywords-full.json is on the known-divergent list - so this is settled as a design decision by the operator, who is reading the physical card. 2026-08-17, WP-568)
- D-24378 - mock-move-context-events-api (RESERVED: records what an incomplete test mock of a FRAMEWORK interface actually costs, and settles how the engine's mock move contexts model boardgame.io's `EventsAPI`. Locks: (1) a mock that implements a framework interface PARTIALLY is not a smaller mock, it is an untyped one - these literals were structurally invalid for as long as `EventsAPI` has had five members the engine does not use, and nothing noticed because engine test files were never compiled before WP-563; (2) the five members the engine must never call (`endPhase`, `endStage`, `pass`, `setActivePlayers`, `setStage`) are stubbed to THROW, not to no-op, so the completed type surface doubles as a runtime assertion of the ARCHITECTURE.md Phase & Turn Transitions rule - the engine changes phase only via `setPhase` and turn only via `endTurn`; a no-op stub would satisfy the compiler while leaving a violation silent, which is precisely the failure D-24372 exists to stop; (3) the raw error count is a MISLEADING size signal - 159 errors resolved to ~17 helper edits because the errors were call sites of per-file helpers, and future backlog rows should size by DEFECT SITE, not by error count; (4) consolidating the ~30 duplicate per-file mock helpers is explicitly NOT this packet - a shared test builder is a different change in kind and carries its own 01.6 trigger. 2026-08-17, WP-569)
- D-24379 - engine-test-possibly-undefined-indexing (RESERVED: settles that the non-null assertion `!` is a TYPE-LEVEL narrowing, not a suppression, and is therefore permitted where `any` / `@ts-ignore` / `@ts-expect-error` are not. Locks: (1) the DISTINCTION - `@ts-ignore` and `@ts-expect-error` delete a diagnostic and leave the underlying claim unexamined, and `any` deletes the type itself; `!` makes a specific, narrow, checkable claim (this index is populated) that the runtime still enforces, because the expression is dereferenced either way and an undefined value throws. In a TEST the claim is additionally self-proving: the suite already passes while dereferencing these expressions unguarded; (2) `!` is ERASED at compile time and has no runtime semantics, so a mechanical rewrite of index accesses is provably behavior-preserving - which is what makes a regex sweep acceptable here and NOT acceptable for changes that alter emitted code; (3) the TWO idioms and when each applies: inline `!` for a one-off access, and a single `assert.ok(value !== undefined, '<full sentence>')` when the indexed value is bound and read repeatedly, since the assertion narrows once and fails with a message rather than a bare TypeError; (4) `noUncheckedIndexedAccess` is NEVER relaxed to clear this class - the flag is precisely what surfaced 209 unexamined index assumptions, and turning it off would delete the finding rather than address it. Evidence standard: all 209 sites were classified by SOURCE LINE at draft time (154 `playerZones[...]`, 17 array-index, 38 other) and the dominant shape was mechanically rewritten in the largest file to measure the real yield - 498 -> 362 gate, 209 -> 73 class, from one file. 2026-08-17, WP-570)
- D-24380 - engine-test-fixture-builders (RESERVED: records that an engine test fixture for a required-field type is built through a SHARED BUILDER, never a bare object literal, and why the evidence for that is unusually strong. Locks: (1) the failure is RECURRING, not incidental - `git log -S` at draft time dates `faceDownCards` to WP-282 and `horrors` to WP-156, separate packets months apart, and at least SIX required-field additions each left the test fixtures structurally invalid; nothing compiled them, so nothing said so. Fixing the 195 sites inline would restore the exact preconditions for a seventh; (2) a builder supplies the canonical default for every required field and accepts a partial override, so the NEXT required field is added in ONE place and every fixture inherits it - the property that makes this class not recur; (3) defaults are READ from the production type and its setup code, never invented, because a wrong default silently changes what dozens of tests assert - a builder that lies is worse than the literals it replaced; (4) the finding is that the FIXTURES are wrong, never that the types are too strict - no production type is widened or relaxed to accept a partial fixture, which is the same boundary D-24378 defends for `CardRegistryReader`; (5) the builder must be shown to EARN ITS KEEP by mutation - adding a new required field breaks exactly one place - or it is 195 inline edits wearing a helper's clothes. Extends the WP-569 precedent that shared engine test-support lives in a `src/test/` sibling module and that its presence in `dist` is an accepted, enumerated cost. 2026-08-17, WP-571)
- D-24381 - ast-aware-fixture-migration (RESERVED: records that a fixture migration is driven by the TYPE CHECKER, never by source pattern-matching, and that a builder's guarantee is conditional on migration COMPLETENESS. Locks: (1) WP-571 measured the failure precisely - a regex codemod hit TYPE ANNOTATIONS and emitted syntax errors across 102 files, a second regex broke 18 tests by adding keys that failed deep-equality assertions, and an error-driven pass structurally CANNOT see already-complete literals, so the AC-2 mutation broke six places instead of one; (2) the correct oracle is `checker.getContextualType` on each `ObjectLiteralExpression`, which resolves the target types precisely AND distinguishes `Record<string, T>` from `T` - verified by probe at draft time, 230 literals across 59 files, 173 already routed and 57 not; (3) the builder property is conditional on completeness, not on the builder existing - this is the clause D-24380 was amended to say, and the AC that proves it is a MUTATION requiring exactly one break, never a count of fixed errors; (4) some narrow fixtures must STAY incomplete and erroring - the deliberately-narrow registry mocks prove reader guards FIRE, and completing them silences the behaviour under test, so the migration carries an explicit skip-list rather than treating every diagnostic as a defect. 2026-08-17, WP-572)
- D-24382 - ui-projection-fixture-builders (RESERVED: extends D-24380 and D-24381 to the UI-projection family and records that the arc's fixture pattern is now settled rather than re-litigated per packet. Locks: (1) UI-projection fixtures are BUILT, not literal, on the same terms as the six engine state types - a builder per type with canonical defaults read from the production type, so the next required field is added in ONE place; (2) the acceptance is the MUTATION and never the error count, per D-24381 section 3 - WP-571 cut its class 88% while delivering no property, and WP-572's mutation is what proved the difference; (3) the checker-driven migration is the settled mechanism after WP-572 ran it with zero syntax errors where a regex produced 102 broken files, so no future fixture packet re-opens that choice; (4) the deliberately-narrow skip-list posture carries forward unchanged - a fixture that proves a guard FIRES must stay incomplete, and not every diagnostic is a defect. 2026-08-17, WP-573)
- D-24383 - master-strike-bystander-capture-log (RESERVED: records that a deliberately-specified log line can still leave a state change invisible, and that the fix is additive rather than a decision amendment. Locks: (1) a Master Strike's Bystander capture is LOGGED on success, not only on the empty-supply skip - D-15401 specified the empty case and said nothing about the success case, so the silence was a gap in what the decision COVERED rather than a violation of it, and D-15401 stays Immutable and unamended; (2) the evidence standard - the gap was found by reconciling a real match's victory pile against its log (3 Bystanders rescued, 1 capture logged) rather than by reading the code, which is why it survived: nothing in the source looks wrong, and the asymmetry is only visible once the RESCUE line is compared against the missing CAPTURE lines; (3) observability-only - no change to when or whether a Bystander is captured, and both hash oracles stay byte-unchanged because the sentinel excludes messages (D-24081) and the empty replay fires no strike. 2026-08-17, WP-574)
- D-24384 - diagnostics-report-carries-no-effect-trace (RESERVED: records that a diagnostic surface can be fully wired, fully working, and still answer none of the questions it was built for. Locks: (1) an empty `entries` buffer on a clean match is CORRECT and is not the defect - the capture installs at boot and wraps console plus window errors, and its call sites are failure paths, so an error-free session legitimately produces zero entries; recording this explicitly because the obvious reading ('the buffer is broken') would send an executing session to fix working code; (2) the defect is that the report carries the CONSOLE channel while the channel that records what each played card dispatched - `G.diagnostics.traces[]`, WP-488 / D-24294 - is never projected and so never reaches the report; (3) any trace projection stays READ-ONLY and inert - D-24294's rule that no move / rule / endIf / bot / scoring path consumes traces survives, so a projection may never become a gameplay input; (4) evidence standard - found by reading TWO exported reports (a 12-turn 1p and a 24-turn 2p match) against what each was supposed to explain, not by reading the capture code, which looks correct because it IS correct. 2026-08-18, WP-575)
- D-24385 - civil-war-2p-hero-count-requirement (RESERVED: records that Super Hero Civil War's printed "use only 4 Heroes at 2 players" is enforced on the REQUIREMENT side, completing the split WP-515/D-24328 deliberately deferred. Locks: (1) `resolveEffectiveHeroCount` returns 4 for `core/super-hero-civil-war` at exactly 2 players - the single hero-count source that the registry composition checker, the game-engine `matchSetup.validate` gate, and the registry-viewer builder all read, so requirement / validation / UI agree; this is a requirement OVERRIDE (like Secret Invasion's "6 Heroes", D-24337), the OPPOSITE class from a post-validation downsize; (2) the number is EXACTLY 4, not a 4-or-5 range - a 5-hero 2p Civil War loadout is now invalid, matching the printed card and the already-shipped 4-hero built deck; (3) the WP-515/D-24328 engine downsize (`resolveEffectiveHeroDeckIds` `slice(0,4)`) is RETAINED as a defensive no-op now that validation guarantees 4 ids at 2p - it is not removed, so a non-validated build path stays safe, and its stale "requires exactly 5" comment is corrected; (4) evidence standard - the defect was found live: the cards builder grayed out the LAGN download at the card-correct 4 heroes, the "displays 5, plays 4" caveat D-24328 named as deferred. Supersedes the requirement-side half of D-24328's deferral; D-24328's downsize decision stays Active. 2026-08-17, WP-576)
- D-24386 - red-skull-strike-interactive-ko-choice (RESERVED: Red Skull's Master Strike upgrades from the D-24188 both-players-auto-pick to the D-24284 SPLIT. Locks: (1) the ACTIVE player interactively CHOOSES their KO'd Hero - a hand-scoped reuse of the shipped `ko-hero` pending choice [park when >=2 hand Heroes; forced auto-KO at 1; no-op at 0]; (2) NON-ACTIVE allies auto-pick the lowest-cost hand Hero, UNCHANGED [`selectRedSkullKoTarget`]; (3) a BOT current player auto-resolves BYTE-IDENTICAL to the old pick [lowest-cost hand, tie -> lowest index] so all-bot sims + both hash oracles are unchanged; (4) REUSE, don't fork - no new move / pending kind / UIState field / prompt component; the printed zone is HAND-ONLY, enforced by an additive optional `PendingKoHeroChoice` marker that leaves villain ko-hero unchanged. Supersedes the INTERACTIVE half of D-24188 [its auto-pick remains the ally + bot fallback; D-24188 stays Active as the fallback record]. Full non-active-player interactivity remains DEFERRED - it is the first non-active-player interactive choice in the engine and needs the play phase `activePlayers` restructured [boardgame.io rejects a non-active seat's move today] + a multi-entry projection. Evidence standard: found live in a 2p Red Skull / Super Hero Civil War match review. 2026-08-19, WP-577)
- D-24387 - surface-competitive-score-on-endgame (RESERVED: records that the competitive score is server-derived and therefore surfaced client-side, not engine-populated. Locks: (1) D-6701's premise stands - the engine has no `ReplayResult` at gameover, so `buildParBreakdown` STAYS returning `undefined` and `UIGameOverState.par` is NOT populated in `buildUIState`; the score reaches the player from the SERVER's submit/score response, rendered in `EndgameSummary`; (2) the endgame score is a READ-ONLY presentation of what the server already computed (`parScoring.logic.ts`) and stored - the client never recomputes rawScore/parScore/finalScore; (3) guests (no submit) and pending/failed submits degrade gracefully to the existing outcome+VP summary with no score, never an error; casual/unranked matches still show the score; (4) no engine, persistence, or hash surface changes. Found in a real 2p match review. 2026-08-21, WP-578)
- D-24388 - leaderboard-handle-profile-link (RESERVED: locks the leaderboard identity display. (1) the leaderboard shows the CLAIMED handle (`display_handle`) LINKED to the public profile `play.legendary-arena.com/?profile=<handle_canonical>`, replacing today's plain-text `display_name`; (2) FALLBACK for a ranked player who never claimed a handle is settled at draft - render `display_name` as plain text with no link, never drop the row; (3) whether the email-local-part `display_name` fallback may keep reaching the public board is settled here (privacy); (4) an additive, versioned snapshot-schema change carries the handle through `legends.types.ts` + the builders; score computation, ranking, eligibility and the 5-min publisher cadence are untouched. 2026-08-21, WP-579)
- D-24389 - use-recruit-as-attack-conversion (RESERVED: introduces the engine's first resource-conversion primitive. Locks: (1) a new hero keyword marks "use recruit as attack this turn" - the ability was invisible because it carried no `[keyword:]` token (only `[icon:]` decoration) and `parseAbilityText` mapped it to two undefined-magnitude no-op grants that `detectHollowHeroHook` did not flag; the marker + keyword enrollment fix both; (2) a TURN-SCOPED economy flag set onPlay and CLEARED in `resetTurnEconomy` governs conversion - no permanent state; (3) SPEND-ORDER is attack-first-then-convertible-recruit, applied uniformly to `fightVillain`, `fightMastermind`, the bot affordability projection (`rules/ai.legalMoves.ts`) and the UIState economy projection (available attack = availableAttack + flag?availableRecruit:0), so bot and player see the same affordability (legalMoves<->move-guard mirror); (4) SCOPE = core God of Thunder ONLY, one-directional, whole-turn - msp1/cvwr(fight-gated)/co2e(any-amount)/xmen(X-Gene bidirectional) DEFERRED, each its own follow-up; (5) DETERMINISM - a new hash-bearing `TurnEconomy` field either takes a `finalStateHash` + `PRE_WP080_HASH` dual re-pin or is kept turn-reset/lazy to move neither oracle, decided at execution and proven. Found live: God of Thunder played twice, conversion never offered. 2026-08-21, WP-580)
- D-24390 - recruit-as-attack-visible-cue (RESERVED: locks that WP-580's recruit-as-attack conversion gets a VISIBLE, ACCESSIBLE cue rather than staying a silent economy change. (1) the EXISTING `G.turnEconomy.recruitSpendableAsAttack` flag is projected onto `UITurnEconomyState` (a UIState projection, NOT a new G field - no hash surface), omit-when-absent, threaded through the active-player-only audience filter with the drift pin extended; (2) the cue is DISPLAY-ONLY - it changes nothing about how the conversion computes or spends (WP-580 / D-24389 behavior byte-identical); (3) the cue is accessible - non-color-only, aria-labelled, reduced-motion-respecting - per Vision section 17; (4) rationale: the WP-580 live-verify surfaced that a passive economy change is undiscoverable ("no option provided") even when working. 2026-08-21, WP-581)
- D-24391 - rogue-copy-powers-full-duplicate (RESERVED: SUPERSEDES D-24345 Fork 2 ["'Copy' = RE-FIRE the chosen Hero's on-play ability ... NOT its printed stats ... does NOT re-add the copied Hero's base attack/recruit economy"]. The operator confirmed from the physical Marvel Legendary card that Rogue's Copy Powers is a FULL DUPLICATE of the copied Hero - attack, recruit, hero class, effects AND team - not ability-only. Locks: (1) `applyCopyPowers` adds the copied instance's printed attack + recruit (`G.cardStats[chosenHeroId]` via `addResources`) on BOTH resolution paths; the copied Hero was already played this turn, so the copy is a SECOND instance and legitimately DOUBLES the stat (intended); (2) Copy Powers counts as the copied Hero's TEAM for hero team-synergy via a new lazy `G.cardCopiedTeams` map + `getGrantedTeams` helper routed through `heroConditions.evaluate.ts requiresTeam`, mirroring the D-24345 class grant and reaching the SAME single hero-synergy read; (3) CLASS (`cardSizeChangingClasses`, D-24074) + ABILITY (the `executeHeroEffects` re-fire) are UNCHANGED - already faithful; (4) NON-GOAL: full team/class parity across the villain/scheme/mastermind/tactic/defeat-req/UIState read sites (the class grant does not reach them either) is a deferred consistency follow-up; (5) DETERMINISM: `turnEconomy` delta is a real gameplay change; the team map is lazy/omit-when-empty; no committed fixture plays Copy Powers -> both hash oracles byte-identical, no re-pin. Found live: Copy Powers cloned Cyclops' Unending Energy and did not add its printed attack. D-24345's ability + class + interactive-choice + recursion/parse fix-forwards all STAY Active; only Fork 2's "ability only, not stats" clause is superseded. 2026-08-22, WP-582)
- D-24392 - endgame-score-breakdown-and-grade (RESERVED: locks the endgame score-breakdown + grade surface. (1) the FULL ScoreBreakdown is displayed on the endgame screen from the ALREADY-returned record.scoreBreakdown [the server builds via buildScoreBreakdown + persists jsonb + returns from both score endpoints; the client MyCompetitiveScore type merely omits it] - NO server/persistence/migration change, extend the client type + render; (2) a GRADE is a pure shared engine helper gradeForFinalScore [ScoreGrade union + SCORE_GRADES drift-pinned array in scoring/parScoring.grade.ts, barrel-exported], mirroring menaceTierFor/MENACE_TIERS - the engine ships the ENUM ['legendary'|'a'|'b'|'c'|'d'|'f'], the client owns the display word "Legendary" [the D-24367/D-24371 no-copy-in-packages boundary]; (3) grade bands on the PAR-relative finalScore [centesimal, lower=better, 0=par]: legendary<=-1000, a<=-300, b<=300, c<=800, d<=1800, else f - operator-approved at draft, tunable via the helper constant; (4) breakdown rendered VERBATIM [never recomputed client-side, preserving WP-578/D-24387]; (5) endgame screen ONLY - a leaderboard/legends grade badge is a deferred follow-up [the shared engine helper makes it a trivial later reuse, no threshold drift]; (6) ACCESSIBLE [not colour-only, aria-labelled, reduced-motion]; (7) NO hash/G/persistence/server change - a pure additive engine helper + client display. Rationale: a real 2p Red Skull/Midtown win surfaced that raw 920 / competitive 1220 are opaque - the player cannot see WHERE the score came from or how good it is. 2026-08-22, WP-583)
- D-24393 - endgame-score-worked-calculation (RESERVED: extends D-24392's endgame surface to render the competitive score as a WORKED SOLUTION rather than a flat component list. (1) FORMULA-FIRST [operator choice]: a symbolic formula line [Raw = (Rounds × 50) + Penalties − (Bystanders × 200) − (VP × 10)], then substituted values, products, raw; then Final = Raw − PAR = final → Grade; (2) WHOLE numbers [operator choice - centesimal integers as shown today, no ÷100]; (3) per-term weights are DERIVED from record.scoreBreakdown [product÷count] never hardcoded client-side, so the shown formula cannot drift from the engine's weights; a 0-count term degrades gracefully [shows its 0 product]; (4) rendered VERBATIM from the ALREADY-returned record.scoreBreakdown [never recomputed - WP-578/D-24387 preserved]; (5) arena-client ONLY - a pure client display reformat [EndgameSummary.vue + a pure vfx/scoreCalcDisplay.ts helper]; NO engine/server/persistence/hash change; (6) ACCESSIBLE [real text, aria-labelled, not colour-only]. Rationale: WP-583 listed the components but did not show the arithmetic; the operator wants the endgame to show the work "like grading a student's work". 2026-08-22, WP-584)
- D-24394 - rulebook-faithful-scoring-drop-round-cost (RESERVED: makes competitive scoring rulebook-faithful by REMOVING the LA-invented per-round cost from RawScore. The Marvel Legendary v23 "Scoring" section penalizes ONLY -4/bystander-lost, -3/scheme-twist, -1/villain-escaped [the 4:3:1 anchor, VERIFIED against the printed rulebook] and has NO round/turn penalty - twists are its length proxy. LA had layered roundCost [50/round] on top, double-counting length. Locks: (1) FULL removal of roundCost [ScoringWeights] + roundsPar [ParBaseline] + weightedRoundCost [ScoreBreakdown]; RawScore = Penalties - (BP*bystanderReward) - (VP*vpReward); ScoringInputs.rounds KEPT informational [shown as a given, not scored]; (2) bystander/VP rewards + penalty weights UNCHANGED - the deliberate LA moral-hierarchy [Vision 20-26] stays; only the redundant round cost goes; (3) both version pins BUMPED: scoringConfigVersion 2->3, rawScoreSemanticsVersion 1->2 [a formula-shape change]; existing competitive_scores rows keep their pinned par_version/scoring_config_version/score_breakdown -> NO retroactive invalidation [docs/12 calibration invariant]; the rawScoreSemanticsVersion bump is the designed filter for a mixed old/new leaderboard; (4) seed PAR artifacts REGENERATED in-place v1 [every parValue drops by roundsPar*50, every artifactHash changes]; write-once guard requires rm-then-regenerate; a pre-release/uncalibrated seed [Phase-2 sim will replace it] so this is an acceptable exception to the 12.1 immutability doctrine; (5) DETERMINISM: scoring is server-side end-of-match, NOT in the game-state hash -> NO finalStateHash/PRE_WP080 re-pin [PAR artifact hashes DO change - expected]; (6) the server defense-in-depth computeParScore(config)===parValue check stays correct because both recompute from the regenerated artifact - regeneration is MANDATORY or live submissions fail-close. Rationale: operator questioned the twist penalty as luck; the rulebook check found the reverse - twists are the canonical length signal and the LA round cost is the redundant addition [option 1, rulebook-purist]. 2026-08-22, WP-585)
- D-24395 - bystander-rescued-undercount-fix (RESERVED: records the single-source-of-truth fix for the Bystander undercount in competitive scoring. A rescued Bystander reaches a victory pile from TWO ext-id sources - villain-deck bystanders [villainDeckCardTypes[id]===bystander] AND supply-pile bystanders [BYSTANDER_EXT_ID = pile-bystander, never in villainDeckCardTypes]. The scoring-input derivations [deriveScoringInputs, deriveScoringInputsFromFinalState] counted only the first, so the competitive score undercounted rescues and mis-graded runs, while computeFinalScores [VP] and countBystandersRescued [HUD] counted both - three surfaces disagreeing. Locks: (1) ONE exported predicate isBystanderCard(gameState,cardExtId) in scoring.logic.ts is the sole definition of Bystander-in-a-pile, used by computeFinalScores, countBystandersRescued, and BOTH scoring-input derivations [rescued and bystanderLost loops on both the live and PAR-calibration paths]; (2) the live and PAR paths stay symmetric by sharing it - a narrow predicate on either side systematically skews PAR vs the live score; (3) server-side derived from terminal G, adds no G field, so finalStateHash and PRE_WP080_HASH are byte-unchanged [no re-pin - verified by the full engine suite staying green]; (4) existing competitive_scores rows are NOT retroactively recomputed [pinned breakdown preserved, the WP-585 no-retroactive-invalidation precedent]. Rationale: operator reported an endgame score that credited ~8 bystanders against a victory pile of ~18-26; root cause was the missing supply-pile source in the scoring derivation, not the instance-id gotcha. 2026-08-22, WP-586)
- D-24396 - endgame-par-derivation-and-grade-scale (RESERVED: locks the endgame score-legibility surface added by WP-587 - PAR derivation, grade scale, named penalties. (1) the engine `ScoreBreakdown` gains `parBaseline: ParBaseline` [the baseline parScore was derived from], field-copied in buildScoreBreakdown [JSON-serializable, no shared reference, D-2801/D-4806]; a derived, display-only projection [never re-entered into scoring]; the server persists and returns the whole breakdown as jsonb verbatim, so NO server code change and rows persisted before WP-587 simply lack the field [client degrades gracefully]; (2) `SCORE_GRADE_BANDS` [ScoreGrade + inclusive-upper finalScore ceiling, null ceiling on the worst band] is the SINGLE SOURCE OF TRUTH for both classification and the displayed scale; gradeForFinalScore iterates it; runtime drift-pinned against SCORE_GRADES + the boundary table [WP-563 runtime-assertion rule]; (3) the client owns all player-facing words AND the grade-range formatting [gradeDisplay.buildGradeScale] and the penalty NAMES + PAR-derivation strings [scoreCalcDisplay], keeping the no-copy-in-packages boundary [D-24392/D-24367]; the PAR-derivation reuses the match-derived per-unit weights [PAR and raw share config weights] and shows a weight symbolically rather than fabricate one when the match count that would reveal it is zero [e.g. 0 escapes]; (4) NO scoringConfigVersion bump [additive display data, no formula/weight change] and NO game-state-hash re-pin [scoring is server-side end-of-match, adds no G field - verified by the green replay/sentinel suites]. Rationale: operator asked where the PAR value came from, what score each grade needs, and to name the penalties instead of lumping them; layout approved via a show_widget mockup before build. 2026-08-22, WP-587)
- D-24397 - endgame-report-card-refinements (RESERVED: locks the three WP-588 endgame report-card refinements. (1) PER-PLAYER SPLIT: ScoringInputs gains optional `perPlayer` [{playerId, victoryPoints, bystandersRescued}], derived in deriveScoringInputs from terminal G [each player totalVP via computeFinalScores; per-player bystanders via the shared isBystanderCard predicate on that player victory pile], deep-copied through buildScoreBreakdown [JSON-serializable, no shared reference, D-2801/D-4806]. The competitive score REMAINS a shared-team score [D-4803]; perPlayer is a display-only reconciliation that sums to the team victoryPoints + bystandersRescued [never changes the score]. Optional: the synthetic inputs computeParScore builds have no players, and rows persisted before WP-588 carry none -> the report card shows only team totals for them. (2) PAR BASIS: the endgame names what sets PAR - the scenario identity [ScenarioKey = scheme::mastermind::sorted-villain-groups] and the seed calibration [composeScenarioDifficulty = 0.40*mastermind + 0.40*scheme + 0.20*avg(villains)]; i.e. scheme + mastermind + villain groups, explicitly NOT henchmen [the operator premise, corrected against the code]. Client copy only [no packages/ copy]. (3) GRADE SCALE: rendered as a horizontal colour-coded strip [one cell per SCORE_GRADE_BANDS entry, tinted its grade colour, earned cell emphasised]; colour is REINFORCEMENT [the current cell also carries a text marker + aria-current], preserving the D-24392 not-colour-alone rule. Determinism: perPlayer derived from terminal G, adds no G field -> NO finalStateHash/PRE_WP080 re-pin [verified by green replay/sentinel]; no scoringConfigVersion bump [additive display data]; the server returns the breakdown jsonb verbatim so NO server code change. - D-24398 - optional-ko-reward-icon-suppression (RESERVED: locks the parser icon-suppression rule for optional-ko-reward. - D-24399 - optional-ko-reward-ko-log (RESERVED: locks the KO-cost log line for resolveOptionalKoReward. - D-24400 - par-interim-scheme-aware-recalibration (RESERVED: locks the interim scheme-aware PAR recalibration [WP-591] pending simulation calibration. (1) SCHEME-AWARE BASELINES: bystander rescues are a SCHEME property [validated from 13 real-game diagnostics: Midtown 24-37, Secret Invasion 8-17, Civil War 4-7, Cosmic Cube 3-11], so the flat difficulty->baseline template is replaced by per-scheme profiles anchored to observed competent-win medians [structural estimates for the schemes without a game yet]. (2) PHYSICAL BASELINES [option A]: ParBaseline gains schemeTwistsPar + bystandersLostPar and computeParScore subtracts those penalties, so PAR models the same penalties as the raw score and the baseline stays physically meaningful [the WP-587 endgame PAR-derivation display stays honest]. (3) LOSS PENALTY: a flat penalty [~6000 centesimal] is added to rawScore when the match outcome is not heroes-win [ScoringInputs.matchLost, derived server-side-faithfully from evaluateEndgame], so a bystander-heavy LOSS grades D/F and can never out-grade a competent win [operator: grade-by-margin, not auto-F]. (4) RETUNED GRADE BANDS on the PAR-relative final: Legendary<=-2000, A<=-700, B<=700, C<=2000, D<=4000, else F [operator target: solid win=B, exceptional=A/Legendary; validated so Cosmic Cube wins stop grading F and Midtown wins stop pinning Legendary]. (5) VERSIONS: scoringConfigVersion 3->4 + rawScoreSemanticsVersion 2->3 [formula-shape change]; existing competitive_scores rows keep their pinned versions [NO retroactive invalidation]. (6) DETERMINISM: scoring is server-side end-of-match, adds no G field -> NO finalStateHash/PRE_WP080 re-pin; deriveScoringInputs sets matchLost internally -> NO server route change [jsonb pass-through]. This is explicitly an INTERIM: simulation calibration [VISION 26 Phase-2] remains the real answer once the competent AI is strong enough to be a valid competent-play reference [its current 55th-pct is near break-even, far below human]. 2026-08-23, WP-591)
- D-24401 - rogue-steal-abilities-hero-ability (RESERVED: locks the steal-abilities HeroKeyword semantics [WP-592] for Rogue's core/rogue/steal-abilities "Each player discards the top card of their deck. Play a copy of each of those cards." (1) DETERMINISTIC + SYNCHRONOUS: the handler parks NO new pending-choice type of its own -> NO new G field, NO new resolve move, NO arena-client change; it delegates to the existing keyword handlers, whose own interactive choices reuse their existing pending types/prompts/guards [FIFO]. (2) DISCARD PHASE: every player, in seat order 0..numPlayers-1, discards the top card of their deck to their OWN discard pile; an empty deck reshuffles the discard first [reuse reshuffleDiscardIntoDeck, the D-24285 standard]; a player with no cards anywhere discards nothing. (3) COPY PHASE: the Steal Abilities player plays a copy of each discarded card in seat order = its printed economy [G.cardStats attack/recruit; starters carry entries, wounds carry none -> a copied wound adds nothing] PLUS a re-fire of its on-play ability via the reentrant executeHeroEffects, threading the FULL move-context wrapper so a copied draw/reshuffle replays from ctx.random [mirrors applyCopyPowers]. (4) EPHEMERAL COPIES: the copies enter no zone [the real discarded cards stay in their owners' discard piles] and grant Steal Abilities NO class/team [unlike Copy Powers D-24391 - a copy of each card is not a merge into the source card]. (5) RECURSION GUARD: a discarded card whose base ext_id is core/rogue/steal-abilities is NOT re-fired [economy still applies; the copy-ability is suppressed with a logged note], mirroring the COPY_POWERS_EXT_ID self-exclusion [WP-535] that fixed the copy-of-copy stack-overflow server crash; a termination test plays Steal Abilities with a Steal Abilities on top of a deck and asserts no infinite loop. (6) DETERMINISM: ctx.random is used ONLY for the empty-deck reshuffle; the card-data marker restages G.heroAbilityHooks for Rogue games -> scaffold-first to confirm any finalStateHash / sim runtime-observed re-pin, and regenerate ALL card-data-derived artifacts. 2026-08-23, WP-592)

- D-24408 - bystander-scoring-dominance-policy (RESERVED: standalone SCORING-POLICY decision [no WP/EC/code]. Records that bystander rescue is the single largest score lever [rescue -200, loss +400 vs VP -10; a bystander = 20 VP] and that this is WATCHED for meta-homogenization / gamification pressure. Current mitigation = scheme-aware PAR [WP-591/D-24400]: PAR expects each scheme natural bystander count, so rescue-to-par is neutral and the score-optimal loadout VARIES by scheme across the gauntlet -> no card forced gauntlet-wide. Reserved lever IF single-scenario boards actually converge on rescue teams: DIMINISHING-RETURNS-PAST-PAR [each rescue beyond the scheme expected count worth less] preferred over nerfing the weight [protecting bystanders is thematically core]; modest bystander:VP ratio narrow is the blunt alternative. Governance rule locked: score-optimal levers MUST stay reachable from Core/free content [NG-1 no-pay-to-win]. Do NOT reweight on sparse data; revisit only with real ranked-board evidence. 2026-08-24, claude/spec-bystander-scoring-policy)
- D-24409 — rulebook-faithful-scoring-supersedes-24408 (RESERVED: locks WP-599. SUPERSEDES D-24408 [operator decision 2026-08-24: the −200 bystander reward is a non-rulebook invention, not merely a dominance concern]. Full rulebook fidelity: remove the dedicated bystanderReward; rescued bystanders score only as their 1 VP [10]; rescale penalties to true VP-units 10/30/40 [rulebook 4:3:1, community VP−4·lost−3·twists−1·escape]; drop invariants 1&3 [they existed only to prop the invented reward], keep 2 [bystanderLost>villainEscaped, 40>10]; LOSS_PENALTY 6000→600; grade bands re-derived for the compressed scale [interim/tunable]. scoringConfigVersion 4→5 / rawScoreSemanticsVersion 3→4; existing rows keep pinned versions [no retroactive invalidation]. Game-winner VP still counts bystanders [rulebook]. 2026-08-24, worktree-rulebook-faithful-scoring)
